Basket: cavity center around (0.00-0.01, 0.265). The liner top z ≈ 0.09-0.10 and rim z ≈0.12-0.14.

Placement: I want the object to end up centered in the cavity. Target object xy = (0.00, 0.265) → eef xy = (0.00-0.034, 0.265-0.006) = (-0.034, 0.259).

Hmm, but wait: the basket midpoint x from region center at z 0.08-0.16 was 0.007 with median -0.003. The window cols 720-940 correspond to... but does that window include the far rim at col 940? Yes. So basket center x ≈ 0.0.

But hold on — the liner is a fabric insert that appears crumpled; visible "interior" opening inner edges. Let me reconsider: from the image, the basket mouth spans cols ~715 to ~945 and rows ~415 to ~555 (top face). Its world center = (0.00, 0.265).

However, when the bottle drops in, the In predicate for basket in LIBERO: `In(object, basket_1)` uses the basket's region computed from its bbox; the object's center must be within the basket's container region. Placing at the center is safest.

Carry increments: from (-0.108,-0.225) to (-0.034, 0.259): dy = 0.484 — needs 2-3 hops each <0.30 (also smooth). Let's do:
- (-0.09, -0.05, 0.30)
- (-0.05, 0.14, 0.30)
- (-0.034, 0.259, 0.30)
